is an integer.
(That is,
\gamma\vcentcolon=\lceil\mathcal{T}/t\rceil-\mathcal{T}/t
.)
Then, recalling the definition of
N(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})
from (
5.5
),

(K.18)

\displaystyle N(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\geq\#\{j\geq 0:X_{\mathcal{T}+(\gamma+j)t}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\text{ and }\mathcal{T}/t+\gamma+j\leq\lfloor T/t\rfloor-1\}.

Let
J\vcentcolon=\lfloor c_{5}^{\prime}\ell^{2}/t\rfloor
and note that we then have
\Delta=(J+2)t
.
Hence, the assumption that
\mathcal{T}\leq T-\Delta
ensures that
\mathcal{T}/t+\gamma+J\leq T/t-2+\gamma
.
Consequently, since
\mathcal{T}/t+\gamma+J
is an integer and
-2+\gamma\leq-1
, we have
\mathcal{T}/t+\gamma+j\leq\lfloor T/t\rfloor-1
for every
j\leq J
, and hence

(K.19)

\displaystyle N(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\geq\#\{0\leq j\leq J:X_{\mathcal{T}+(\gamma+j)t}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}.

Now, first combining (
K.17
)–(
K.19
) and subsequently using the strong Markovianity from
Proposition

3.3
,

(K.20)

\displaystyle{}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{\|y-p\|:p\in\hat{\mathcal{X}}\}>\mathcal{C}_{3}\ln(T/t)\sqrt{t},\,\mathscr{E}_{\mathrm{\text{good}}}\mid X_{\tau}=y,s_{i}(L^{\mkern-2.0mu{\text{{$($}{\hskip-0.3pt}$i$\hskip-0.3pt{$)$}}}}_{\tau})=\mathcal{S}_{i},\tau=\mathcal{T}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\#\{0\leq j\leq J:X_{\mathcal{T}+(\gamma+j)t}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}<\mathfrak{n}_{0}\mid X_{\tau}=y,s_{i}(L^{\mkern-2.0mu{\text{{$($}{\hskip-0.3pt}$i$\hskip-0.3pt{$)$}}}}_{\tau})=\mathcal{S}_{i},\tau=\mathcal{T}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\bigl(\#\{0\leq j\leq J:X_{(\gamma+j)t}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}<\mathfrak{n}_{0}\mid X_{0}=y,s_{i}(0)=\mathcal{S}_{i}\bigr).

The right-hand side of (
K.20
) can be controlled using
Lemma

5.5
.
The assumptions in (
5.9
) of that lemma are satisfied due to the choice of
\mathcal{C}_{5},\mathcal{C}_{6}
, and
\mathcal{C}_{7}
together with the choice of
p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n}
and
J
, at least if
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
are taken sufficiently small.
Hence, with
N_{+}
as in (
5.11
),

(K.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\#\{0\leq j\leq J:X_{(\gamma+j)t}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}<{}

\displaystyle{}\mathfrak{n}_{0}\mid X_{0}=y,s_{i}(0)=\mathcal{S}_{i}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(N_{+}<\mathfrak{n}_{0})+\eta/4.

We next use
Lemma

5.6
whose assumptions can be satisfied by taking
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
sufficiently small.
10
10

10

In particular, the assumption that
J\geq\tilde{c}_{1}\ln(\tilde{c}_{2}/\eta)^{2}/\eta^{2}
can be equivalently phrased as
\lfloor c_{5}^{\prime}\mathcal{C}_{6}^{2}\ln(T/t)^{2}\rfloor\geq\tilde{c}_{1}\ln(\tilde{c}_{2}/\eta)^{2}/\eta^{2}
and will hence be satisfied if
\mathcal{C}_{1}
is sufficiently small in (
K.5
).

Hence, recalling from (
K.8
) that
\mathfrak{n}_{0}\leq(\tilde{c}_{3}\eta/4)\sqrt{J}
,

(K.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(N_{+}<\mathfrak{n}_{0}\Bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}\Bigl(N_{+}<\frac{\tilde{c}_{3}\eta}{4}\sqrt{J}\Bigr)\leq\eta/4.

Combine (
K.15
), (
K.16
), (
K.20
), (
K.21
), and (
K.22
) to conclude that

(K.23)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{\|X_{\tau}-p\|:p\in\hat{\mathcal{X}}\}>\mathcal{C}_{3}\ln(T/t)\sqrt{t}\text{ and }\tau\leq T\bigr)\leq\eta.

This is equivalent to the desired performance guarantee (
2.12
) by passing to the complementary event, concluding the proof.
∎

K.1.1.
Proof of
Lemma

K.1

As in the proofs of
Lemmas

F.2
and
G.3
in
Sections

F.2
and
G.4
, the argument amounts to an application of the union bound.
Different from the setting of those proofs, however, we are now concerned with a regime where
T
may be small.
This will be exploited to get an estimate which does not degrade when
\operatorname{Area}(D)
is large.

Proof of
Lemma

K.1
.

By the union bound,

(K.24)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\exists j,k,n:\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{good}}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\neq 1\bigr)\leq\sum_{j,k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor 2\pi\ell/\epsilon\rfloor}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{good}}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\neq 1\bigr).

Fix some
j,k,n
and note from (
K.1
) and (
K.2
) that it is only possible to have
\mathcal{E}_{\text{good}}(p,\vec{v})\neq 1
if
N(p,\vec{v}\mkern 2.0mu)\geq\mathfrak{n}_{0}\geq 1
.
Recall from (
5.5
) that this means that
X_{it}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})
for some
i\in\{0,1,\ldots,\lfloor T/t\rfloor-1\}
.
Hence, conditioning on the first visit to
R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})
,

(K.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{good}}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\neq 1\bigr)={}

\displaystyle{}\sum_{i=0}^{\lfloor T/t\rfloor-1}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{u\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}:X_{ut}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}=i\bigr)

\displaystyle{}\ \times\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{good}}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\neq 1\mid\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{u\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}:X_{ut}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}=i\bigr).

Note that
\tau=t\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{u\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}:X_{ut}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}
is a stopping time.
Hence, the strong Markovianity of
Proposition

3.3
implies that
(X_{s+\tau})_{s\geq 0}
is again a reflected Brownian motion with semipermeable barriers.
Applying
Corollary

5.4
to that process, there exist absolute constants
c,C>0
such that

(K.26)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{good}}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\neq 1\mid\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{u\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}:X_{ut}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}=i\bigr)\leq C\exp(-c\mathfrak{n}_{0})

if
c_{1},\ldots,c_{5}
are chosen appropriately in the statement in
Lemma

K.1
.
Further, note that
\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{u\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}:X_{ut}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}=i
can only occur if
X_{it}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}\mkern 2.0mu)
.
In particular, noting in (
5.1
) that every point in
R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}\mkern 2.0mu)
is at distance
\leq\ell+2\sqrt{t}\leq 3\ell
from
p_{j,k}
,

(K.27)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{u\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}:X_{ut}\in R_{+}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\}=i\bigr)\leq\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|X_{it}-p_{j,k}\|\leq 3\ell\bigr).

Given
X_{it}
, there can be at most
(6\ell/\epsilon+1)^{2}
points
p_{j,k}=(\epsilon j,\epsilon k)
with
\|X_{it}-p_{j,k}\|\leq 3\ell
.
Hence, also using that
\epsilon\leq\sqrt{t}/2\leq\ell/2
, there exists an absolute constant
C^{\prime}>0
such that for every fixed
i
,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\lfloor 2\pi\ell/\epsilon\rfloor}\sum_{j,k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{P}\bigl(\|X_{it}-p_{j,k}\|\leq 3\ell\bigr)

\displaystyle=(\lfloor 2\pi\ell/\epsilon\rfloor+1)\mathbb{E}\bigl[\#\{j,k\in\mathbb{Z}:X_{it}\in\mathscr{B}(p_{j,k},3\ell)\}\bigr]

(K.28)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}(\ell/\epsilon)^{3}.

Now, combining (
K.24
)–(
K.28
) there exists an absolute constant
C^{\prime\prime}>0
such that

(K.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\exists j,k,n:\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{good}}(p_{j,k},\vec{v}_{n})\neq 1\bigr)\leq C^{\prime\prime}(T/t)(\ell/\epsilon)^{3}\exp(-c\mathfrak{n}_{0}).

Taking
c_{6}>0
sufficiently large in the assumed lower bound on
\mathfrak{n}_{0}
now ensures that the right-hand side of (
K.29
) is
\leq\eta
, concluding the proof.
∎

K.1.2.
Proof of
Lemma

K.2

Proof of
Lemma

K.2
.

Let
T^{\prime}\vcentcolon=\min\{T,1/\kappa^{2},1/\lambda_{\max}^{2},\rho^{2}\}
and take the constant
c
in the statement of
Lemma

K.2
sufficiently small to ensure that
\Delta+T^{\prime}/2<T
.
Then, first using the weak Markovianity of
Proposition

3.3
to condition on
X_{T-\Delta-c_{1}T^{\prime}}
for some sufficiently small
c_{1}>0
and subsequently applying
Lemma

4.8
, there exists
c_{2}>0
such that

(K.30)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\forall y\in\cup_{i=0}^{m}B_{i}:\|X_{T-\Delta}-y\|\geq c_{2}\sqrt{T^{\prime}}\bigr)\geq 1-\eta/2.

Further, if the constant
c
in
Lemma

K.2
is taken sufficiently small depending on
c_{2}
so that
\Delta
is a sufficiently small multiple of
T^{\prime}
,

(K.31)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\forall s\leq\Delta:\|W_{T-\Delta+s}-W_{T-\Delta}\|<c_{2}\sqrt{T^{\prime}}\bigr)\geq 1-\eta/2.

Recalling that the stochastic differential equation in
Definition

1
implies that the increments of
X_{T-\Delta+s}
agree with those of
W_{T-\Delta+s}
whenever the process is not on a barrier, the combination of (
K.30
) and (
K.31
) implies (
K.4
).
This concludes the proof.
∎

K.2.
Proof of
Corollary

2.6

As was alluded to in (
5.22
) of
Section

5.3
, the idea is to cut the trajectory into
M\geq 1
pieces such that
\sup\{\|X_{t}-X_{jT/M}\|:t\in[jT/M,(j+1)T/M]\}
is small for every
j
with high probability.
That such an
M
exists is immediate from
X_{t}
being a continuous process, but it requires a small additional computation to verify that the dependence on the geometry of the barriers is only through the parameters of
Section

2.2
:

Lemma K.3
.

For every
\varepsilon>0
,
\eta\in(0,1)
, and
T>0
there exists an integer
M\geq 1
depending only on
\varepsilon,\eta,\min\{1/\kappa,1/\lambda_{\max},\rho\}
, and
T
such that

(K.32)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\sup\{\|X_{t}-X_{jT/M}\|:t\in[jT/M,(j+1)T/M]\}\leq\varepsilon,\ \forall j\leq M-1\bigr)\geq 1-\eta.

Proof.

Define a sequence of nonnegative random variables
\tau_{1},\tau_{2},\ldots
by

(K.33)

\displaystyle\tau_{1}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{t\geq 0:\|X_{t}-X_{0}\|>\varepsilon/2\text{ or }s_{i}(L^{\mkern-2.0mu{\text{{$($}{\hskip-0.3pt}$i$\hskip-0.3pt{$)$}}}}_{t})\neq s_{i}(0)\text{ for some }i\leq m\},

(K.34)

\displaystyle\tau_{j+1}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{t\geq 0:\|X_{\tau_{j}+t}-X_{\tau_{j}}\|>\varepsilon/2\text{ or }s_{i}(L^{\mkern-2.0mu{\text{{$($}{\hskip-0.3pt}$i$\hskip-0.3pt{$)$}}}}_{\tau_{j}+t})\neq s_{i}(L^{\mkern-2.0mu{\text{{$($}{\hskip-0.3pt}$i$\hskip-0.3pt{$)$}}}}_{\tau_{j}})\text{ for some }i\leq m\}.

Corollary

3.11
then yields some
t>0
depending on
\varepsilon
and
\min\{1/\kappa^{2},1/\lambda_{\max}^{2},\rho^{2}\}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\tau_{j}>t\mid\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{j-1})\geq 1/2
for every
j
.
In particular, one can then find some
J\geq 1
depending on
\varepsilon,\eta,\min\{1/\kappa,1/\lambda_{\max},\rho\}
, and
T
such that

(K.35)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Bigl(\sum_{j=1}^{J}\tau_{j}>T\Bigr)\geq 1-\eta/2.

(For instance, (
K.35
) can be deduced from

Lemma

5.3
.)
Again by
Corollary

3.11
, there exists some
t^{\prime}>0
depending on
J,\varepsilon,\eta,
and
\min\{1/\kappa,1/\lambda_{\max},\rho\}
such that
\mathbb{P}(\tau_{j}\geq t^{\prime})\leq\eta/(2J)
.
Then, by the union bound,

(K.36)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\tau_{j}\geq t^{\prime},\forall j\leq J)\geq 1-\eta/2.

The combination of (
K.35
) and (
K.36
) yields (
K.32
) if we let
M
be sufficiently large to ensure that
T/M<t^{\prime}
.
(Indeed, note that every interval of length
T/M
can then intersect at most two intervals of the form
[\tau_{j},\tau_{j+1}]
.)
This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Corollary

2.6
.

Lemma

K.3
provides some
M\geq 1
with

(K.37)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\sup\{\|X_{t}-X_{jT/M}\|:t\in[jT/M,(j+1)T/M]\}\leq\varepsilon/4,\ \forall j\leq M-1\bigr)\geq 1-\eta/2.

On the other hand, if we define stopping times
\mathcal{T}_{0},\ldots,\mathcal{T}_{M-1}
by

\mathcal{T}_{j}\vcentcolon=\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{t\geq jT/M:X_{t}\in\cup_{i=0}^{k}B_{i}\}
,
then (
2.12
) from
Theorem

2.5
yields
c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2}^{\prime},c_{3}^{\prime}>0
depending on
\eta
and
M
such that for every
j\leq M-1
,

(K.38)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{inf}\vphantom{\mathrm{sup}}}\{\|X_{\mathcal{T}_{j}}-p\|:p\in\hat{\mathcal{X}}\}\leq c_{3}^{\prime}\ln(T/t)\sqrt{t}\text{ or }\mathcal{T}_{j}>T\bigr)\geq 1-\eta/2M

if
t\leq c_{1}^{\prime}T
and
\ln(T/t)^{4}t\leq c_{2}^{\prime}\min\{T,1/\kappa^{2},1/\lambda_{\max}^{2},\rho^{2}\}
.

Define the constant
c_{1}
in the statement of
Corollary

2.6
by
c_{1}\vcentcolon=c_{1}^{\prime}T
and pick the constant
c_{2}
such that
c_{3}^{\prime}\sqrt{c_{2}}\leq\varepsilon/2
and
c_{2}\leq c_{2}^{\prime}\min\{T,1/\kappa^{2},1/\lambda_{\max}^{2},\rho^{2}\}
.
Then, in particular,
c_{3}^{\prime}\ln(T/t)\sqrt{t}\leq\varepsilon/2
.
We claim that the desired result now follows by combining (
K.37
) with (
K.38
) and using the definition of
\cup_{i=0}^{k}\mathcal{X}_{i}
from (
2.10
) together with the triangle inequality.

To be precise, by the union bound, we can assume with probability
\geq 1-\eta
that the event in (
K.37
) occurs and that the event in (
K.38
) occurs for every
j\leq M-1
.
It remains to show that the event described on the left-hand side of (
2.13
) then follows.
Pick some arbitrary
x\in\cup_{i=0}^{m}\mathcal{X}_{i}
.
We have to show that there exists some
p\in\hat{\mathcal{X}}
with
\|x-p\|\leq\varepsilon
.

The assumption that
x\in\cup_{i=0}^{m}\mathcal{X}_{i}
means that there exists some
i\leq m
and
t_{x}\in[0,T]
with
x\in B_{i}
and
x=X_{t_{x}}
.
Let
j_{x}\leq M-1
be the least integer with
t_{x}\geq j_{x}T/M
.
Then, the interval
[j_{x}TM,(j_{x}+1)TM]
contains at least one time for which the process
X_{t}
is in
\cup_{i=0}^{k}\mathcal{X}_{i}
, implying that
\mathcal{T}_{j_{x}}\leq(j_{x}+1)T/M\leq T
.
In particular, by (
K.38
) and the subsequent discussion regarding the choice of
c_{2}
, there exists some
p_{x}\in\hat{\mathcal{X}}
with

(K.39)

\displaystyle\|p_{x}-X_{\mathcal{T}_{j_{x}}}\|\leq\varepsilon/2.

On the other hand, since
t_{x}
and
\mathcal{T}_{j_{x}}
are both times in the interval
[j_{x}T/M,(j_{x}+1)T/M]
, it follows from (
K.37
) that

(K.40)

\displaystyle\|x-X_{\mathcal{T}_{j_{x}}}\|=\|X_{t_{x}}-X_{\mathcal{T}_{j_{x}}}\|\leq\|X_{t_{x}}-X_{jT/M}\|+\|X_{\mathcal{T}_{j_{x}}}-X_{jT/M}\|\leq\varepsilon/2.

Combine (
K.39
) and (
K.40
) to conclude that
\|x-p_{x}\|\leq\varepsilon
, as desired.

∎

Appendix L
Additional details for
Section

7

We finally provide some supplemental details for the case study.
First, recall that the right-hand side of
Figure

6
zoomed in on a region of the southern valley to keep the key features discussed in the text legible.
The complete picture is displayed in
Figure

11
.

Comparing to the satellite image on the left of
Figure

6
, the main findings in the northern valley are similar to those in the southern valley: impermeable barriers arise from the coastline and the slopes of the valley, while a permeable barrier arises from a river.
One can further see some scattered loose points in the far north and in the west.
These stem from rare excursions into low-density areas and do not necessarily correspond to barriers.

As was mentioned in
Section

7
, the parameters were chosen on an ad-hoc basis.
Specifically, the discretization scale
\epsilon
was approximately 120 meters, and the sensitivity parameter used was
\mathfrak{s}\vcentcolon=6\epsilon
.
This choice was arbitrary and experimentation suggests that the qualitative findings are robust to variations of these parameters.
The truncation parameter
\mathfrak{u}
was mainly a technical convenience to simplify the proofs and is not important for this dataset.
This parameter may be useful if the data suffers from outliers, but otherwise one can simply fix it at a large value or use the non-truncated Wasserstein distance with
\mathfrak{u}=\infty
.

Figure 11
.
Full image for the barriers uncovered by
Algorithm

1
.
